1. nce-s-eth-001 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · informed consent and its required elements · easy · In review

A newly licensed counselor is drafting an informed consent document for a private practice. Which element is a required part of informed consent under the ACA Code of Ethics?
A. A guarantee that the presenting problem will improve within a set number of sessions
Counselors explain potential benefits and risks; promising a specific outcome is misleading.
B. An explanation of the limits of confidentiality and how fees and billing are handled ✔ KEY
A.2.b lists confidentiality limits and fees and billing arrangements among required disclosures.
C. A summary of the counselor's personal religious and political values for the client
Counselors avoid imposing personal values (A.4.b); disclosing them is not a consent element.
D. A signed waiver releasing the counselor from liability for any outcome of treatment
Liability waivers are not part of informed consent and do not serve client welfare.
Rationale: Informed consent gives clients the information needed to choose whether to enter or continue counseling. ACA A.2.b names elements such as the purposes, goals, techniques, risks and benefits of services, the counselor's credentials, fees and billing, the limits of confidentiality, and the client's right to refuse services. Consent is an ongoing process, not a single signature.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.2.b Types of Information Needed

2. nce-s-eth-002 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · informed consent and its required elements · medium · In review

A 12-year-old is brought to counseling by his custodial grandmother, who holds legal guardianship. The boy says he does not want to be there. How should the counselor BEST approach consent?
A. Treat the grandmother's signature as sufficient and skip any discussion with the boy
Legal consent from a guardian does not remove the duty to seek the minor's assent.
B. Decline to begin services until the boy is old enough to provide legal consent alone
Minors routinely receive counseling with guardian consent; refusing service is not required.
C. Let the boy decide alone whether to continue, since his preference overrides consent
A minor generally cannot give legal consent; the guardian's role cannot simply be set aside.
D. Obtain the guardian's consent and seek the boy's assent in language he can understand ✔ KEY
A.2.d directs counselors to seek the assent of clients who cannot give consent and to include guardians.
Rationale: When a client cannot legally consent, counselors obtain consent from the parent or guardian and still seek the client's assent, explaining services in developmentally appropriate terms. This balances the guardian's legal rights with the minor's autonomy and welfare. Age-of-consent rules for minors vary by state, so counselors also check state law.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.2.d Inability to Give Consent
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

3. nce-s-eth-003 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · confidentiality and its limits · medium · In review

A counselor is starting a grief group for adults. During the first session, what should the counselor tell members about confidentiality?
A. That members are asked to keep what is shared private, but this cannot be guaranteed ✔ KEY
B.4.a asks counselors to explain the importance and parameters of confidentiality in group work.
B. That group confidentiality is protected by the same law that binds the counselor
Group members are generally not bound by the counselor's legal duties; this overstates protection.
C. That confidentiality applies only to the counselor, so members may share freely
Members should be asked to respect each other's privacy; this undermines group safety.
D. That any member who discloses outside the group will face legal action by the counselor
This is an inaccurate threat; the counselor cannot enforce group confidentiality this way.
Rationale: In group work the counselor can promise to protect confidentiality but cannot guarantee that other members will. ACA B.4.a requires counselors to clearly explain the importance and parameters of confidentiality for the specific group. Setting this expectation at the outset supports informed consent and group trust.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.4.a Group Work
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

4. nce-s-eth-004 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · confidentiality and its limits · hard · In review

A client discloses a new diagnosis of a contagious, life-threatening disease and says she has not told her long-term partner, with whom she continues to have unprotected sex. State law permits disclosure in this situation. What should the counselor do FIRST?
A. Contact the partner right away and inform him of the diagnosis without telling the client
B.2.c calls for assessing the client's intent to inform the partner before any disclosure.
B. Keep the information confidential, since health conditions are outside the limits
B.2.c recognizes that disclosure to an identifiable third party at high risk may be justified.
C. Assess whether the client intends to inform her partner or to change the risky behavior ✔ KEY
B.2.c directs counselors to assess client intent before disclosing to third parties at risk.
D. Refer the client to a medical provider and end counseling until she discloses to him
Ending services as leverage is coercive and may amount to abandonment (A.12).
Rationale: ACA B.2.c states that when clients disclose a contagious, life-threatening disease, counselors may be justified in disclosing to identifiable third parties at serious and foreseeable risk if laws permit. Before doing so, counselors assess the client's intent to inform those parties or to stop harmful behavior. State law varies, so counselors confirm what their jurisdiction permits or requires.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.2.c Contagious, Life-Threatening Diseases
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

5. nce-s-eth-005 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · duty to warn and protect · easy · In review

During a session, a client names a former coworker, states he has bought a handgun, and describes a plan to shoot her at her home this weekend. Which action is MOST consistent with the counselor's ethical duties?
A. Keep the threat confidential and explore the client's anger at the next session
A specific, imminent threat to an identifiable person is a recognized limit of confidentiality.
B. Ask the client to sign a no-harm contract and continue with the planned session work
No-harm contracts do not reduce risk and do not meet the duty to protect an identified victim.
C. Take protective steps such as warning the victim or police, as state law directs ✔ KEY
B.2.a permits disclosure to protect identified others from serious and foreseeable harm.
D. End the session early and refer the client to a psychiatrist for medication review
Referral alone does nothing to protect the identified person from imminent harm.
Rationale: Following Tarasoff v. Regents of the University of California (1976), counselors may have a duty to protect identifiable victims of serious threats. ACA B.2.a states that confidentiality does not apply when disclosure is required to protect identified others from serious and foreseeable harm. Whether the duty is mandatory or permissive, and what steps satisfy it, varies by state law.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.2.a Serious and Foreseeable Harm and Legal Requirements · State law — Duty-to-warn/protect statutes (mandatory vs permissive, Tarasoff)
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

6. nce-s-eth-006 · Counseling and Helping Relationships · duty to warn and protect · medium · In review

A client frustrated about a custody dispute says, "Sometimes I could just kill my ex." He names no plan, has no weapons, has no history of violence, and laughs it off. What is the counselor's BEST response?
A. Explore the statement further to assess intent, plan, means, and history of violence ✔ KEY
An ambiguous statement calls for violence risk assessment before any decision to disclose.
B. Notify the ex-spouse and local police immediately that a threat has been made
Breaching confidentiality without assessing seriousness or foreseeability is premature.
C. Ignore the remark, since venting about an ex-spouse is common in custody conflicts
Dismissing a statement of violence without assessment risks missing real danger.
D. Terminate services because the client has made a threat against another person
Termination does not address risk and may leave the client and others less safe.
Rationale: The duty to protect is triggered by serious and foreseeable harm, not by every angry remark. The counselor should assess intent, plan, means, target, and history, consult as needed, and document the reasoning. If assessment reveals a serious threat, protective action under B.2.a and state law follows.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.2.a Serious and Foreseeable Harm and Legal Requirements
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

7. nce-s-eth-007 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · mandated reporting of abuse and neglect · medium · In review

A school-based counselor notices bruises in the shape of a hand on a 7-year-old's arm. The child says, "Dad gets mad," and then stops talking. The counselor is unsure whether abuse occurred. What should the counselor do?
A. Gather more evidence by questioning the parents before deciding whether to report
Investigating is the role of child protective services and may place the child at risk.
B. Wait to see whether new injuries appear before making a report to the authorities
Delay is not permitted once reasonable suspicion exists; certainty is not required.
C. Ask the child's teacher to make the report so the counseling relationship is protected
In most states the duty to report is personal and cannot be handed off to a colleague.
D. Make a report to child protective services based on reasonable suspicion of abuse ✔ KEY
Mandated reporters report reasonable suspicion; proof or certainty is not required.
Rationale: All U.S. states have mandated reporting laws for suspected child abuse and neglect that cover counselors, though definitions, thresholds, and procedures vary by state. The threshold is reasonable suspicion or cause to believe, not proof, and the counselor should not investigate. Specific timelines, reporting agencies, and procedures vary by state law, and many school policies add internal notification steps.
References: State law — Child abuse reporting statutes: reasonable suspicion standard · ACA Code of Ethics — B.2.a Serious and Foreseeable Harm and Legal Requirements
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

8. nce-s-eth-008 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · HIPAA and FERPA basics for counselors · hard · In review

A counselor in a HIPAA-covered agency keeps process notes about session dynamics and her own impressions in a file separate from the medical record. Under the HIPAA Privacy Rule, how are these notes treated?
A. They are part of the designated record set and may be released for any payment purpose
Separately kept psychotherapy notes are excluded from routine payment disclosures.
B. They are psychotherapy notes, and most disclosures require a specific authorization ✔ KEY
HIPAA defines psychotherapy notes and requires specific authorization for most uses.
C. They lose all protection once a client signs a general release of medical information
A general release does not suffice; psychotherapy notes need their own authorization.
D. They must be given to the client on request under the HIPAA right of access to records
Psychotherapy notes are excluded from the HIPAA right of access, though state law may differ.
Rationale: HIPAA gives psychotherapy notes, meaning a counselor's notes analyzing session content that are kept separate from the medical record, special protection. Most uses and disclosures require a specific authorization, and they are excluded from the client's general right of access. Items such as diagnosis, medications, session times, and treatment plans are not psychotherapy notes.
References: HIPAA — Privacy Rule: psychotherapy notes definition and authorization (45 CFR 164.501, 164.508)
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

9. nce-s-eth-009 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · record keeping and documentation · easy · In review

A competent adult client asks to see a copy of her counseling records. What does the ACA Code of Ethics direct the counselor to do?
A. Provide reasonable access, limiting it only with compelling evidence of harm to her ✔ KEY
B.6.e directs reasonable access, limited only when access would cause the client harm.
B. Refuse the request, since counseling records belong only to the counselor and agency
Clients have a right of reasonable access to their own records under B.6.e.
C. Release the records only after she obtains a court order or a lawyer makes a request
No court order is needed for a competent client to access her own records.
D. Give her a summary only, because clients may not see the counselor's written records
The Code does not limit clients to summaries; it supports access and help interpreting records.
Rationale: ACA B.6.e states that counselors provide reasonable access to records and copies when requested by competent clients. Access is limited only when there is compelling evidence that it would cause harm to the client, and counselors document the request and their reasoning. Counselors also offer assistance in interpreting records (B.6.f).
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.6.e Client Access
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

10. nce-s-eth-010 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · multiple relationships and boundary crossings · medium · In review

Two years after ending counseling with a client, a counselor runs into the former client at a community event, and the former client asks the counselor out on a date. Under the ACA Code of Ethics, what applies?
A. A romantic relationship is allowed now, since more than one year has passed since the end
The ACA standard is five years, not one year.
B. A romantic relationship is allowed if a colleague agrees there is no risk of exploitation
Consultation does not override the five-year prohibition.
C. A romantic relationship is prohibited for five years after the last professional contact ✔ KEY
A.5.c prohibits such relationships for five years following the last professional contact.
D. A romantic relationship is permitted as long as the former client initiated the request
Who initiates does not change the prohibition; responsibility rests with the counselor.
Rationale: ACA A.5.c prohibits sexual or romantic relationships with former clients, their romantic partners, or family members for five years after the last professional contact, in person or electronic. Even after five years, counselors must show forethought and document that the relationship is not exploitative. The responsibility rests with the counselor regardless of who initiates.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.5.c Sexual and/or Romantic Relationships With Former Clients
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

11. nce-s-eth-011 · Counseling and Helping Relationships · multiple relationships and boundary crossings · medium · In review

A counselor in a small rural town is invited by a long-term client to attend the client's high school graduation, which the client has worked toward despite major barriers. The counselor believes attending could benefit the client. What should the counselor do?
A. Decline, because any contact outside the office is an ethical violation for counselors
The Code permits boundary extensions that benefit clients when handled with safeguards.
B. Weigh benefits and risks, discuss it with the client, and document the rationale ✔ KEY
A.6.b asks counselors to document rationale, benefit, and anticipated consequences.
C. Attend without discussion, since a graduation is a public event open to everyone
A boundary extension should be discussed and documented, not done casually.
D. Attend only if the client agrees to end counseling before the graduation ceremony
Terminating services to permit contact is unnecessary and harms the client.
Rationale: ACA A.6.b recognizes that extending boundaries, such as attending a formal ceremony, can benefit clients. Before doing so, counselors document the rationale, potential benefit, and anticipated consequences, and take precautions such as informed consent, consultation, and supervision. Rural settings make such decisions more common, which increases the need for careful reasoning.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.6.b Extending Counseling Boundaries
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

12. nce-s-eth-012 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · scope of practice and competence · hard · In review

A licensed counselor completes a one-day introductory workshop on a specialized trauma therapy and wants to start using it with several clients who have complex trauma. Which course of action BEST meets the counselor's ethical obligations?
A. Use the method now, since the counselor's license covers all trauma treatments
A license defines legal scope, not competence in every specialized method.
B. Use the method now but tell clients it is experimental and get written consent
Consent does not replace the training needed to use a new specialty competently.
C. Use the method now, since a workshop certificate shows enough competence to practice
A single introductory workshop rarely provides the training and supervision C.2.b requires.
D. Pursue further training and supervised practice before using it, and refer if needed ✔ KEY
C.2.b requires education, training, and supervised experience before practicing a new specialty.
Rationale: ACA C.2.a limits practice to the boundaries of competence based on education, training, supervised experience, and credentials. When entering a new specialty area, C.2.b requires appropriate education, training, and supervised experience, and steps to protect clients from harm while skills develop. Referral is appropriate when the counselor cannot yet provide competent care.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — C.2.b New Specialty Areas of Practice · ACA Code of Ethics — C.2.a Boundaries of Competence
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

13. nce-s-eth-013 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · ACA Code of Ethics structure · easy · In review

A counselor wants guidance on keeping separate professional and personal social media profiles. Which section of the 2014 ACA Code of Ethics MOST directly addresses this issue?
A. Section B, Confidentiality and Privacy
Section B covers privacy broadly but social media is addressed in Section H.
B. Section D, Relationships With Others
Section D addresses relationships with colleagues and other professionals.
C. Section C, Professional Responsibility
Section C covers competence, advertising, and credentials, not social media profiles.
D. Section H, Distance Counseling ✔ KEY
Section H covers distance counseling, technology, and social media, including H.6.
Rationale: The 2014 ACA Code has nine sections, A through I. Section H, Distance Counseling, Technology, and Social Media, was new in 2014; H.6.a directs counselors who keep both a professional and a personal presence on social media to create separate profiles. Knowing the section layout helps counselors find guidance quickly.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — Section H; H.6.a Virtual Professional Presence
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

14. nce-s-eth-014 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · NBCC Code of Ethics · medium · In review

A licensed counselor who also holds the National Certified Counselor (NCC) credential is accused of an ethics violation. Which statement about the NBCC Code of Ethics is accurate?
A. It replaces the state licensing board, so state law no longer applies to the counselor
State licensure law applies independently of national certification.
B. It binds NCCs, and NBCC can act on the certification apart from any state board action ✔ KEY
NBCC enforces its Code for its certificants; boards enforce licensure separately.
C. It applies only to counselor educators who teach in CACREP-accredited graduate programs
The NBCC Code applies to NCCs and applicants, not only to educators.
D. It is advisory only, so NBCC cannot suspend or revoke a credential for a violation
NBCC can impose sanctions on certification, including revocation.
Rationale: The NBCC Code of Ethics applies to National Certified Counselors and applicants for NBCC credentials. NBCC can investigate complaints and sanction or revoke certification, which is separate from any action a state licensing board takes on a license. A counselor may therefore answer to more than one body for the same conduct.
References: NBCC Code of Ethics — Scope and application to NCCs and applicants; enforcement of certification
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

15. nce-s-eth-015 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · ethical decision-making models · medium · In review

While evaluating a chosen course of action, a counselor asks, "Would I be comfortable if this decision were reported in the local newspaper?" Which test from Stadler's model is the counselor applying?
A. The test of justice
Justice asks whether the counselor would treat others in the same situation the same way.
B. The test of universality
Universality asks whether the counselor would recommend the action to others in the same situation.
C. The test of publicity ✔ KEY
Publicity asks whether the counselor would be comfortable having the action made public.
D. The test of fidelity
Fidelity is a moral principle about keeping commitments, not one of Stadler's tests.
Rationale: Many ethical decision-making models, including the Forester-Miller and Davis practitioner's guide, include Stadler's three tests for evaluating a selected action. Justice asks whether others would be treated the same, publicity asks whether the counselor would accept public reporting, and universality asks whether the counselor would recommend it to colleagues. Passing all three supports the soundness of the decision.
References: Forester-Miller & Davis (Practitioner's Guide to Ethical Decision Making) — Step 7, evaluating the selected course of action: Stadler's tests of justice, publicity, and universality
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

16. nce-s-eth-016 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · supervision and consultation responsibilities · hard · In review

A clinical supervisor learns that a post-master's supervisee has not told clients that she is under supervision or that their cases are discussed with the supervisor. What is the supervisor's MOST appropriate response?
A. Require the supervisee to inform clients of her status and how supervision affects privacy ✔ KEY
F.1.c requires that clients know supervisee qualifications and supervision's effect on confidentiality.
B. Allow it, since supervision is internal and does not change confidentiality for clients
Case discussion with a supervisor is a disclosure clients must be told about.
C. Report the supervisee to the licensing board before discussing the problem with her
Corrective supervision is the first step; a board report is premature here.
D. End the supervision contract at once and tell the supervisee to find a new supervisor
Abrupt termination leaves clients unprotected and skips remediation.
Rationale: A supervisor's primary obligation is to monitor the welfare of the clients served by the supervisee (F.1.a). Supervisors ensure that clients are aware of the supervisee's qualifications and of how the supervision process affects the limits of confidentiality (F.1.c). Supervisors share responsibility for client care and should correct the problem promptly through supervision.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — F.1.c Informed Consent and Client Rights · ACA Code of Ethics — F.1.a Client Welfare
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

17. nce-s-eth-017 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · licensure, credentialing and professional identity · easy · In review

Which statement BEST describes the difference between state licensure and national certification for counselors?
A. Licensure is legal permission to practice in a state; certification is a voluntary credential ✔ KEY
Licensure is state regulation; certifications such as the NCC are voluntary and national.
B. Certification is required by law in every state; licensure is optional for private practice
This reverses the two; licensure is the legal requirement for independent practice.
C. Licensure and certification are the same credential, issued by different agencies by region
They are distinct credentials with different legal weight and issuing bodies.
D. Certification allows practice in all states; licensure applies only to school counselors
National certification does not authorize practice in any state.
Rationale: Licensure is a state legal credential that regulates the practice and title of professional counseling within that jurisdiction. National certification, such as the NBCC's National Certified Counselor credential, is voluntary and shows that a counselor met national standards but does not by itself permit practice. Interstate practice is addressed through state law and mechanisms such as the Counseling Compact.
References: Gladding (Counseling: A Comprehensive Profession) — Credentialing: licensure, certification, and accreditation

18. nce-s-eth-018 · Social and Cultural Diversity · counselor advocacy and social justice · medium · In review

A counselor learns that a client who uses a wheelchair is being denied a reasonable accommodation by her landlord. The counselor wants to contact the housing authority about this client's case. What must the counselor do FIRST?
A. Contact the housing authority right away, since advocacy is a core professional duty
Advocating for an identifiable client without consent breaches confidentiality.
B. Avoid advocacy entirely, because it falls outside the counselor's professional role
A.7.a affirms advocacy to address barriers to client growth and development.
C. Write a public letter about the landlord to raise awareness of housing discrimination
Publicizing the case exposes the client and still lacks her consent.
D. Obtain the client's consent before advocating on her behalf as an identifiable client ✔ KEY
A.7.b requires client consent before advocating on behalf of an identifiable client.
Rationale: ACA A.7.a supports advocacy at individual, group, institutional, and societal levels to address barriers that inhibit client growth. A.7.b requires counselors to obtain client consent before engaging in advocacy on behalf of an identifiable client. Seeking consent also respects the client's autonomy and may support her own self-advocacy.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.7.b Confidentiality and Advocacy

19. nce-s-eth-019 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · telehealth and technology ethics · medium · In review

A counselor licensed in one state is asked to provide video counseling to a client who lives in another state. Which consideration is MOST important before beginning?
A. Only the laws of the counselor's home state apply, since that is where the license is
H.1.b notes that the client's state laws may also apply.
B. The counselor may be subject to the laws of both the counselor's and client's locations ✔ KEY
H.1.b directs counselors to know the laws of both locations in distance counseling.
C. Licensure rules do not apply to video sessions, since no in-person contact takes place
Distance counseling is still the practice of counseling and is regulated.
D. Any license is valid nationwide for telehealth as long as the platform is encrypted
Encryption addresses security, not legal authority to practice across states.
Rationale: ACA H.1.b states that counselors providing distance services understand they may be subject to the laws and regulations of both their practicing location and the client's place of residence. In practice, many states regulate based on where the client is located, and rules vary by state. Interstate options such as the Counseling Compact apply only among member states.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — H.1.b Laws and Statutes · Counseling Compact — Privilege to practice in member states
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

20. nce-s-eth-020 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · telehealth and technology ethics · easy · In review

A counselor is starting video sessions with a new client who has a history of suicidal ideation. Which step is MOST important to complete during the first telehealth session?
A. Ask the client to keep the camera off so that the session feels less intrusive
Turning off video reduces the counselor's ability to assess and does not address safety.
B. Record the session so it can be reviewed later if any emergency happens
Recording requires consent and does nothing to prepare for an emergency.
C. Confirm the client's location and a local emergency plan and contact person ✔ KEY
Knowing the client's location and local resources is essential for responding to a crisis.
D. Use any free video app the client prefers so that the client feels comfortable
Platforms must protect confidentiality; preference alone is not the standard (H.2.d).
Rationale: Distance counseling requires planning for emergencies the counselor cannot handle in person. Counselors confirm the client's physical location, identify local emergency services, and agree on a plan as part of telehealth informed consent (H.2.a). This is especially important for clients with elevated risk.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — H.2.a Informed Consent and Disclosure (includes emergency procedures when the counselor is unavailable)
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

21. nce-s-eth-021 · Social and Cultural Diversity · termination and referral · medium · In review

A counselor with strong religious beliefs is assigned a client who wants help with stress in her same-sex marriage. The counselor feels uncomfortable and wants to refer the client elsewhere. What does the ACA Code of Ethics state?
A. Referral is appropriate whenever a client's issue conflicts with the counselor's faith
A.11.b prohibits referral based solely on the counselor's personally held values.
B. Referral is required, since counselors must avoid any client whose values differ
The Code calls for respecting diversity and avoiding value imposition, not avoiding clients.
C. Referral based only on the counselor's personal values is not permitted by the Code ✔ KEY
A.11.b prohibits values-based referral; counselors seek training to work with diverse clients.
D. Referral is acceptable if the counselor explains the religious reason to the client
Explaining the reason does not make a values-based referral ethical and may harm the client.
Rationale: ACA A.11.b states that counselors refrain from referring prospective and current clients based solely on the counselor's personally held values, attitudes, beliefs, and behaviors. A.4.b directs counselors to avoid imposing their own values. Counselors seek training and supervision when values affect their work, and referral is reserved for genuine gaps in competence.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.11.b Values Within Termination and Referral · ACA Code of Ethics — A.4.b Personal Values
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

22. nce-s-eth-022 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · counselor impairment and self-care · hard · In review

A counselor notices that a colleague has been arriving late, smelling of alcohol, and falling asleep in team meetings. The colleague carries a full caseload. What should the counselor do FIRST, if it can be done safely?
A. Speak privately with the colleague about the concerns and encourage seeking help ✔ KEY
C.2.g and I.2.a support helping colleagues recognize impairment and informal resolution first.
B. File a complaint with the state licensing board without speaking to the colleague
Formal reports follow when informal steps fail or clients face imminent harm.
C. Tell the colleague's clients about the concerns so that they can make choices
This breaches the colleague's privacy and is not the counselor's role.
D. Say nothing, since a colleague's personal life is outside the counselor's concern
The Code expects counselors to act when colleague impairment may harm clients.
Rationale: ACA C.2.g asks counselors to monitor themselves for impairment and to assist colleagues or supervisors in recognizing their own impairment, providing consultation and help when warranted. I.2.a directs counselors to first attempt informal resolution with a colleague when feasible. If the problem persists or clients are at substantial risk, counselors take further action, such as reporting to supervisors or the licensing board.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — C.2.g Impairment · ACA Code of Ethics — I.2.a Informal Resolution

23. nce-s-eth-023 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · professional organizations and their roles · easy · In review

A student comparing master's programs in clinical mental health counseling asks which organization accredits counseling graduate programs. Which organization is it?
A. NBCC
The National Board for Certified Counselors (NBCC) grants national certification (such as the NCC) and administers the NCE.
B. CACREP ✔ KEY
The Council for Accreditation of Counseling and Related Educational Programs (CACREP) accredits master's and doctoral counseling programs.
C. ACA
The American Counseling Association (ACA) is a professional membership association that publishes the Code of Ethics.
D. AMHCA
The American Mental Health Counselors Association (AMHCA) is a professional association for clinical mental health counselors.
Rationale: CACREP, the Council for Accreditation of Counseling and Related Educational Programs, accredits counseling graduate programs. NBCC certifies individual counselors and administers the NCE, while ACA and its divisions and affiliates, such as AMHCA and ASCA, are membership associations. State licensing boards regulate practice within each state.
References: CACREP 2024 Standards — Accreditation of counseling master's and doctoral programs · Gladding (Counseling: A Comprehensive Profession) — Professional organizations and accreditation
☐ Approve☐ Revise☐ RetireNotes:

24. nce-s-eth-024 · Research and Program Evaluation · ACA Code of Ethics structure · medium · In review

A counselor researcher wants to study help-seeking and plans to briefly mislead participants about the study's true aim, because full disclosure would bias their answers. Under the ACA Code of Ethics, when is this acceptable?
A. Whenever participants have signed a general consent form for the overall study
General consent does not by itself justify deception.
B. Whenever the researcher believes the study results will be published widely
Publication plans do not justify deceiving participants.
C. Whenever participants are students, since course credit offsets any harm done
Students need added protection, and credit does not offset deception.
D. Only if no alternative is feasible, the value justifies it, and debriefing follows ✔ KEY
G.2 permits deception only when alternatives are not feasible, value justifies it, and debriefing occurs.
Rationale: The ACA Code allows deception in research only when alternative procedures are not feasible and the prospective value of the research justifies it. Deception that is likely to cause physical or emotional harm is not permitted, and researchers explain the deception during debriefing as soon as feasible. Institutional review boards also review such designs.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — G.2 Rights of Research Participants (deception and debriefing)
⚑ Check: Cited as ACA G.2 generally; confirm subsection letters for deception and debriefing.

25. nce-s-eth-025 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · informed consent and its required elements · medium · In review

A client in private practice stopped attending sessions and has an unpaid balance of several hundred dollars. The counselor is considering turning the account over to a collection agency. What should the counselor do FIRST?
A. Inform the client of the intended collection step and give a chance to pay the balance ✔ KEY
A.10.d requires counselors to inform clients of intended collection actions and offer an opportunity to pay first.
B. Send the account to the agency right away, since the client has already ended counseling
Ending services does not remove the duty to notify the client before using collection measures.
C. Write off the debt, because counselors may not pursue payment from any current or past client
The Code permits collection agencies or legal measures when clients are informed first.
D. Give the agency the client's diagnosis and session notes so it can justify the charges
Only the minimum information needed for collection is disclosed; clinical details are not shared.
Rationale: Under A.10.d, counselors who intend to use collection agencies or legal measures for nonpayment first tell the client what they intend and give the client an opportunity to pay. Fee and billing arrangements, including how nonpayment is handled, also belong in the initial informed consent (A.2.b). Any disclosure to a collector is limited to what is needed.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.10.d Nonpayment of Fees

26. nce-s-eth-026 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · multiple relationships and boundary crossings · medium · In review

In a rural farming community where trading services is common, a client who recently lost his job asks whether he could repair the counselor's office roof in exchange for sessions. Under the ACA Code of Ethics, when is bartering acceptable?
A. Whenever the counselor sets the value of the work, since the counselor bears responsibility
One-sided valuation by the counselor increases the risk of exploitation.
B. Only when the counselor proposes it first, so that the client does not feel pressured to offer
The Code says the client should request the arrangement, not the counselor.
C. If the client requests it, it is not exploitive, it is accepted locally, and it is in writing ✔ KEY
A.10.e lists client request, no exploitation or harm, and community acceptance, with a written contract.
D. Only for clients with no insurance, as long as a supervisor signs off on the trade in advance
Insurance status and supervisor approval are not the conditions the Code sets for bartering.
Rationale: A.10.e allows bartering only when the client requests it, the arrangement does not result in exploitation or harm, and such arrangements are an accepted practice among professionals in the community. Counselors consider cultural implications, discuss concerns with the client, and document the agreement in a clear written contract.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.10.e Bartering

27. nce-s-eth-027 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · termination and referral · easy · In review

A counselor accepts a job in another state and will close her practice in two months. Several of her clients are still working on active treatment goals. Which action BEST avoids client abandonment?
A. Stop scheduling sessions now and mail each client a letter after the office has closed
Ending contact abruptly without preparation or referral is a form of abandonment.
B. Keep seeing clients until the last day and leave referrals to the clients' own judgment
Counselors help arrange continued care rather than leaving clients to find it alone.
C. Ask clients to keep texting her after the move so that their counseling can continue informally
Informal contact across state lines raises licensure and boundary problems, not continuity.
D. Tell clients early, provide pretermination counseling, and help arrange suitable referrals ✔ KEY
A.11.c and A.12 call for timely notice, pretermination counseling, and referral to other providers.
Rationale: A.12 prohibits abandoning or neglecting clients and requires arrangements for continuation of treatment during interruptions such as relocation. A.11.c and A.11.d direct counselors to provide pretermination counseling, recommend other providers, and coordinate an appropriate transfer of services.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.12 Abandonment and Client Neglect · ACA Code of Ethics — A.11.c Appropriate Termination

28. nce-s-eth-028 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · confidentiality and its limits · hard · In review

A counselor receives a subpoena from the attorney for a client's former spouse, demanding the client's full counseling file for a custody hearing. The client has not signed a release. What is the counselor's BEST course of action?
A. Send the complete file at once, since a subpoena carries the same force as a court order
An attorney-issued subpoena generally does not by itself authorize release of privileged records.
B. Contact the client, seek legal advice, and release nothing without consent or a court order ✔ KEY
B.2.d and state privilege law favor client consent or a judicial order, with disclosure limited.
C. Ignore the subpoena, because counseling records are privileged and cannot be required
Ignoring a subpoena can bring legal penalties; privilege must be asserted, not assumed.
D. Call the former spouse's attorney to discuss the client's progress and resolve it informally
Discussing the client with the opposing attorney is itself a breach of confidentiality.
Rationale: A subpoena is a demand that must be answered, but it is not the same as a judge's order to disclose. Counselors notify the client, consult an attorney, and assert privilege when appropriate. Under B.2.d, even when a court orders disclosure, counselors seek the client's written consent or ask that disclosure be prohibited or limited as narrowly as possible. State law varies.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.2.d Court-Ordered Disclosure · State law — Privileged communication and response to subpoenas vs court orders
⚑ Check: Verify that the subpoena-vs-court-order distinction is stated accurately enough for a general (non-state-specific) NCE item.

29. nce-s-eth-029 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · confidentiality and its limits · medium · In review

Three months after a long-term client dies of natural causes, her adult son calls the counselor and asks for copies of his mother's session notes so he can "understand her better." What should guide the counselor's response?
A. Release the notes, since confidentiality obligations end once a client has died
B.3.f states that counselors protect the confidentiality of deceased clients.
B. Release the notes, since any adult child is the legal heir to a parent's own records
Family relationship alone does not confer legal authority over a decedent's records.
C. Protect her confidentiality, following state law on who may act for the deceased ✔ KEY
B.3.f protects deceased clients' confidentiality consistent with law and agency policy.
D. Share a verbal summary instead, since a summary does not breach confidentiality
A summary is still a disclosure of confidential information without valid authorization.
Rationale: B.3.f directs counselors to protect the confidentiality of deceased clients, consistent with legal requirements and agency or setting policies. Typically only a legally authorized personal representative, such as an executor, may authorize release, and who qualifies is set by state law, which varies.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.3.f Deceased Clients · State law — Personal representative authority over a deceased person's records

30. nce-s-eth-030 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · supervision and consultation responsibilities · easy · In review

A licensed counselor wants advice from a peer consultation group about a difficult case involving a well-known local business owner. The client has not given consent to discuss the case. How should the counselor proceed?
A. Present the case without details that could reasonably identify the client to the group ✔ KEY
B.7 bars identifying disclosures in consultation without consent, sharing only what is needed.
B. Share the client's name, since members of a consultation group are bound by confidentiality
Peer consultants do not share the counselor's duty in a way that permits identifying the client.
C. Avoid consultation altogether, since any discussion of a case breaches client confidentiality
Consultation is encouraged; it is done with de-identified information or with consent.
D. Share full details but ask the group to sign a confidentiality agreement before the meeting
A group agreement does not replace client consent for identifying disclosures.
Rationale: In case consultation, counselors do not disclose information that could reasonably lead to the client's identification unless the client has consented or disclosure cannot be avoided, and they share only what is needed for the consultation. With a well-known client, extra care is needed to remove identifying details.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.7 Case Consultation

31. nce-s-eth-031 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · confidentiality and its limits · medium · In review

A counselor is about to begin couples counseling with two partners in their fifties. To prevent problems later, what should the counselor do regarding confidentiality at the outset?
A. Promise each partner that anything said in an individual call will stay secret from the other
Blanket secret-keeping promises can undermine the couple work and should not be made by default.
B. Tell both partners that the counselor will decide case by case what to reveal between them
An unannounced, case-by-case approach leaves clients unable to give informed consent.
C. Skip the topic, since confidentiality rules do not apply when both partners are present
B.4.b requires discussing confidentiality expectations and limits in couples work.
D. Define who the client is and get a written agreement on how confidences will be handled ✔ KEY
B.4.b asks counselors to define the client and document agreement on confidentiality.
Rationale: In couples and family counseling, B.4.b directs counselors to clearly define who is considered the client and to discuss expectations and limits of confidentiality. Counselors seek agreement among all parties, document it in writing, and A.8 adds that the nature of each relationship is clarified at the outset.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.4.b Couples and Family Counseling · ACA Code of Ethics — A.8 Multiple Clients

32. nce-s-eth-032 · Group Counseling and Group Work · scope of practice and competence · easy · In review

A counselor is forming a 10-week psychoeducational group on managing workplace stress. According to the ACA Code of Ethics, what is the main purpose of screening prospective members?
A. To make sure the group is full before the first meeting so the program stays funded
Enrollment targets are not the ethical purpose of screening.
B. To select members whose goals fit the group and whose well-being will not be at risk ✔ KEY
A.9.a calls for selecting compatible members who will not impede the group or be harmed.
C. To collect enough personal history that members need not share anything in sessions
Screening is about fit and safety, not replacing disclosure during the group.
D. To exclude any applicant who has a mental health diagnosis from a psychoeducation group
A diagnosis alone is not a reason to exclude; fit with group goals is the standard.
Rationale: A.9.a directs counselors to screen prospective group members to the extent possible, selecting members whose needs and goals are compatible with the group's goals, who will not impede the group process, and whose well-being will not be jeopardized by the group experience.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.9.a Screening · Yalom & Leszcz (Group Psychotherapy) — Selection of clients for group therapy

33. nce-s-eth-033 · Group Counseling and Group Work · counselor advocacy and social justice · medium · In review

During the fourth session of an adolescent anger-management group, several members begin mocking a quieter member's accent, and she starts to cry. What is the leader's MOST appropriate response?
A. Intervene at once to stop the mocking and protect the member, then process what happened ✔ KEY
A.9.b requires reasonable precautions to protect group members from emotional harm.
B. Let the group handle it alone, since peer feedback is how adolescent groups mature over time
Allowing ridicule to continue fails the leader's duty to protect members.
C. Ask the upset member to leave until she is calm enough to rejoin the group discussion
Removing the targeted member punishes her rather than addressing the harmful behavior.
D. Note the incident and raise it with each member's parents after the session has ended
Delaying action leaves the member unprotected during the session.
Rationale: In a group setting, A.9.b directs counselors to take reasonable precautions to protect clients from physical, emotional, or psychological trauma. Blocking harmful interactions is a core leader skill; once safety is restored, the incident can be processed, including the bias it reflects.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.9.b Protecting Clients · Yalom & Leszcz (Group Psychotherapy) — Leader role in establishing group norms and safety

34. nce-s-eth-034 · Assessment and Testing · scope of practice and competence · easy · In review

An agency director asks a newly licensed counselor to administer and interpret an individually administered intelligence test. The counselor has never been trained on this instrument. What should the counselor do?
A. Administer it after reading the manual, since the license covers all forms of assessment
Reading a manual does not establish competence with a complex individual test.
B. Administer it but have the computer scoring report serve as the clinical interpretation
Counselors remain responsible for interpretation even when scoring is automated (E.9).
C. Decline until trained and supervised in its use, and refer the testing to a qualified peer ✔ KEY
E.2.a limits counselors to assessments they are trained and competent to use.
D. Administer it, since the director has the authority to approve staff for any test that is needed
An employer cannot confer competence; the ethical duty rests with the counselor.
Rationale: E.2.a states that counselors use only those testing and assessment services for which they have been trained and are competent. Counselors who rely on technology-assisted interpretation are trained in the construct and the instrument before using it. Until competent, the counselor refers the assessment.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — E.2.a Limits of Competence · Hays (Assessment) — Test user qualifications

35. nce-s-eth-035 · Assessment and Testing · record keeping and documentation · hard · In review

A client involved in a disability claim signs a release asking the counselor to send her raw personality test responses and scores directly to her attorney, who has no training in psychological testing. How should the counselor respond under the ACA Code of Ethics?
A. Send the raw data, since a signed release obligates the counselor to send anything asked for
Consent is necessary but E.4 also limits release to qualified recipients.
B. Refuse any release, since test results may not leave the counselor's office in any form
Results can be released with consent; the issue is who receives raw data.
C. Send the raw data only after the attorney promises not to share it with the opposing side
A promise of nondisclosure does not make an untrained recipient qualified to interpret.
D. Offer a written interpretive report, or send raw data to a professional qualified to use it ✔ KEY
E.4 releases identified data with consent only to persons qualified to interpret them.
Rationale: E.4 states that counselors release assessment data in which the client is identified only with consent of the client or a legal representative, and only to persons the counselor recognizes as qualified to interpret the data. A report written for lay readers, or release to a qualified professional, meets both the client's need and the standard.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — E.4 Release of Data to Qualified Personnel · HIPAA — Individual right of access to the designated record set

36. nce-s-eth-036 · Assessment and Testing · scope of practice and competence · medium · In review

A counselor plans to use a depression inventory with a client who immigrated from Vietnam a year ago and is learning English. The inventory was normed mainly on U.S.-born English speakers. What is the BEST ethical approach?
A. Use the inventory as usual, since depression symptoms appear the same across all cultures
Symptom expression and item meaning can differ across cultures and languages.
B. Use it with caution, note its norm limits, and weigh results with interviews and context ✔ KEY
E.8 calls for cautious use of measures normed on other groups and cultural context.
C. Translate the items on the spot and score the client's answers with the published norms
An informal translation changes the test, so the published norms no longer apply.
D. Skip all formal assessment, since no standardized test can be fair to a recent immigrant
Assessment can be used with appropriate caution; avoiding it entirely is not required.
Rationale: E.8 directs counselors to select and use with caution assessment techniques normed on populations other than the client's, and to recognize the effects of culture, language, and other factors on administration and interpretation. Results are placed in the context of other information, and limits are noted in the report.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — E.8 Multicultural Issues/Diversity in Assessment · Hays (Assessment) — Culturally fair assessment and norm-group relevance

37. nce-s-eth-037 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · supervision and consultation responsibilities · hard · In review

A site supervisor has given a post-master's supervisee written feedback for six months about missed risk assessments and late notes. Despite a written remediation plan, the problems continue. What is the supervisor's MOST appropriate next step?
A. Document the deficits, consult, and decline to endorse licensure while following due process ✔ KEY
F.6.b supports remediation, consultation, and withholding endorsement with due process.
B. Endorse the supervisee for licensure anyway, since all of the required hours have been completed
Hours alone do not justify endorsing a supervisee who is not competent.
C. Quietly end supervision and let the supervisee find another supervisor without a record
Ending without documentation or due process sidesteps the gatekeeping role.
D. Report the supervisee to the licensing board for misconduct without telling the supervisee
Performance deficits call for documented gatekeeping steps, not a secret misconduct report.
Rationale: Supervisors act as gatekeepers. Under F.6.b, they identify limitations that could impede performance, help secure remediation, and when needed recommend dismissal or decline to endorse supervisees who are unable to provide competent services. They consult, document their decisions, and ensure the supervisee knows the options for addressing the decision.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — F.6.b Gatekeeping and Remediation

38. nce-s-eth-038 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · supervision and consultation responsibilities · medium · In review

A counselor educator teaching a group counseling course requires students to take part in an experiential group as members. How should the educator handle evaluation of students in this experience?
A. Grade each student on how much personal material the student discloses in the group
Basing grades on depth of self-disclosure is what the Code prohibits.
B. Share notable disclosures with faculty so they can assess students' fitness for practice
Students should know the risks of self-disclosure; casual sharing of it misuses the group.
C. Keep grades apart from the level of self-disclosure and explain the risks of disclosing ✔ KEY
Section F requires that evaluation not depend on self-disclosure and that risks be explained.
D. Require that students disclose a past trauma, since depth is needed for group learning
Requiring specific personal disclosures exploits students and is not required to learn.
Rationale: When educational programs include self-growth or self-disclosure experiences, counselor educators inform students of the ethical ramifications of self-disclosure, and evaluation is not dependent on the student's level of self-disclosure. Many programs also use outside facilitators so the instructor does not evaluate what students reveal.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — Section F, self-growth experiences in counselor education · CACREP 2024 Standards — Experiential group learning in the group counseling core area

39. nce-s-eth-039 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · telehealth and technology ethics · easy · In review

After a session in which a new client mentions a popular online video channel she runs, the counselor is tempted to look up the channel and the client's public social media posts. What does the ACA Code of Ethics advise?
A. Viewing is fine, since anything posted publicly by a client is open to the counselor
Public availability does not override the Code's respect for client privacy online.
B. Viewing is fine if the counselor does not comment, like, or follow the client's posts
Passive viewing without consent still intrudes on the client's virtual presence.
C. Viewing is required, since it gives collateral information useful for diagnosis
There is no duty to search clients online, and doing so needs consent.
D. Respect the client's online privacy and view it only if the client gives consent ✔ KEY
H.6.c directs counselors to respect clients' social media privacy unless given consent.
Rationale: H.6.c directs counselors to respect the privacy of clients' presence on social media unless given consent to view such information. Counselors also explain their own social media policies as part of informed consent (H.6.b).

40. nce-s-eth-040 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · record keeping and documentation · easy · In review

A counselor in solo practice is retiring and has paper files for clients seen over the past 15 years. How should the counselor handle retention and disposal of these records?
A. Discard all files in the office recycling bin once the practice has officially closed
Recycling bins expose confidential records and ignore retention requirements.
B. Keep files for the period state law requires, then destroy them in a secure manner ✔ KEY
B.6.h links retention to law and requires disposal that protects confidentiality.
C. Give each file to a family member to store at home until the clients ask for them
Untrained family members lack authority and safeguards for client records.
D. Shred all files at once, since a closed practice has no further duty for its records
Retention duties continue after closing; immediate destruction may violate law.
Rationale: B.6.h directs counselors to store records after termination to ensure reasonable future access, to maintain them in accordance with federal and state law and licensure rules, and to dispose of them in a way that protects confidentiality, such as shredding. Retention periods are set by state law and vary. Counselors also plan for records in case of retirement or incapacity.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — B.6.h Storage and Disposal After Termination · State law — Record retention periods for licensed counselors

41. nce-s-eth-041 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · ethical decision-making models · medium · In review

A counselor at a state agency is told that a new agency rule requires sharing full session notes with a client's employer whenever the employer pays for services. The counselor believes this conflicts with the ACA Code of Ethics. What should the counselor do FIRST?
A. Follow the rule without comment, since a legal requirement replaces any ethical code
I.1.c first calls for stating the ethical commitment and trying to resolve the conflict.
B. Make known the commitment to the Code and take steps to resolve the conflict ✔ KEY
I.1.c directs counselors to state their commitment and work to resolve the conflict.
C. Refuse to follow the rule, since ethical codes take legal priority over agency rules
Ethical codes do not override law; outright refusal skips the steps the Code sets out.
D. Stop taking notes so that there is no record that could be given to employers
Failing to document violates record-keeping duties and does not resolve the conflict.
Rationale: Under I.1.c, when ethical responsibilities conflict with laws, regulations, or other legal authority, counselors make known their commitment to the ACA Code and take steps to resolve the conflict. If it cannot be resolved that way, counselors, acting in the client's best interest, may adhere to the legal requirement.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — I.1.c Conflicts Between Ethics and Laws

42. nce-s-eth-042 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · licensure, credentialing and professional identity · hard · In review

A counselor learns that a colleague has been billing insurers for family sessions that never took place. When she raised it privately, the colleague dismissed her concern and the billing continued. What should the counselor do NEXT?
A. Report it to the licensing board or another proper authority, since informal efforts failed ✔ KEY
I.2.b calls for formal reporting when informal resolution fails or harm is likely.
B. Raise it with the colleague again every month, since informal resolution must still be continued
The Code does not require repeating failed informal efforts while harm continues.
C. Warn the colleague's clients about the billing so that they can decide to change counselors
Contacting the colleague's clients is not an appropriate reporting channel.
D. Drop the matter, since billing disputes are between insurers and providers, not counselors
Fraud is an ethical violation counselors are expected to address.
Rationale: I.2.a supports informal resolution first when appropriate. When an apparent violation has substantially harmed or is likely to harm others and is not appropriate for, or not resolved by, informal resolution, I.2.b directs counselors to take further action such as referral to state licensure boards, ethics committees, certification bodies, or institutional authorities.
References: ACA Code of Ethics — I.2.b Reporting Ethical Violations · ACA Code of Ethics — I.2.a Informal Resolution

43. nce-s-eth-043 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · licensure, credentialing and professional identity · medium · In review

A licensed professional counselor holds a master's in counseling and a doctorate in music history. On her practice website she calls herself "Dr." and lists her credentials. What does the ACA Code of Ethics say about this?
A. It is acceptable, since any earned doctorate allows the title "Dr." in every context
C.4.d restricts using "Dr." in a counseling context when the doctorate is unrelated.
B. It is acceptable if the website also lists her current state counseling license number too
A license number does not correct a misleading implication of doctoral-level competence.
C. It is acceptable as long as no client has ever complained that the title was misleading to them
Misrepresentation is unethical whether or not anyone complains.
D. It is misleading, because the doctorate is not in counseling or a closely related field ✔ KEY
C.4.d bars implying doctoral-level competence with an unrelated doctorate.
Rationale: C.4.a requires accurate representation of credentials, and C.4.d states that counselors do not imply doctoral-level competence by using "Dr." in a counseling context when their doctorate is not in counseling or a related field. C.3.a likewise requires advertising to be accurate and not misleading.

44. nce-s-eth-044 · Professional Counseling Orientation and Ethical Practice · confidentiality and its limits · hard · In review

A 78-year-old client with a terminal illness tells his counselor he is considering ending his life through his state's legal medical aid-in-dying process. He has no other risk factors. Under the ACA Code of Ethics, how should the counselor approach confidentiality?
A. Disclose the plan to his family at once, since any talk of dying requires a breach of trust
B.2.b gives counselors discretion; automatic disclosure is not required.
B. Arrange involuntary hospitalization, since his choice must be treated as suicidal
A lawful end-of-life choice is not automatically treated as a psychiatric emergency.
C. Use discretion on breaching, guided by consultation, law, and the client's situation ✔ KEY
B.2.b gives counselors discretion, informed by consultation and applicable law.
D. Refer him to another counselor, since end-of-life choices fall outside counseling
Supporting end-of-life decision making is within the counselor's role.
Rationale: B.2.b gives counselors who work with terminally ill clients considering hastening death the option to maintain confidentiality, depending on applicable laws and the specific circumstances, after seeking consultation or supervision from appropriate professional and legal parties. Counselors also help clients explore options, values, and supports. State law on medical aid in dying varies.

45. nce-s-eth-045 · Social and Cultural Diversity · counselor advocacy and social justice · medium · In review

A 24-year-old client from a close-knit Mexican American family lives at home and gives part of his pay to his parents. The counselor, who values independence, begins urging him to move out to "become his own person." The client has not named this as a goal. What is the ethical concern?
A. The counselor is right to push independence, since adulthood requires leaving home
This is a culturally bound assumption, not a universal developmental requirement.
B. The counselor is imposing personal values instead of exploring the client's own ✔ KEY
A.4.b bars imposing the counselor's values; goals should reflect the client's worldview.
C. The counselor should refer him, since cultural differences make counseling unworkable
Cultural difference is not grounds for referral; the counselor builds competence instead.
D. The concern is billing, since family issues are not covered as a reason for counseling
Billing is unrelated to the ethical issue of value imposition.
Rationale: A.4.b directs counselors to be aware of, and avoid imposing, their own values, attitudes, and beliefs. Many collectivist families see interdependence and financial support of parents as healthy. Goals should come from the client, explored within the client's cultural context (A.2.c, A.1.c).
References: ACA Code of Ethics — A.4.b Personal Values · Sue & Sue (Counseling the Culturally Diverse) — Individualism vs collectivism and culture-bound counseling values
